    39→def _setup_telemetry() -> None:
    40→    """Configure OpenTelemetry tracing for the Cohezion API."""
    41→    resource = Resource.create(
    42→        {
    43→            "service.name": "cohezion-api",
    44→            "service.version": "1.0.0",
    45→            "deployment.environment": os.environ.get("COHEZION_ENV", "development"),

    56→            provider.add_span_processor(BatchSpanProcessor(ConsoleSpanExporter()))
    57→
    58→    trace.set_tracer_provider(provider)
    59→

    64→

    67→
    68→
    69→@contextlib.asynccontextmanager
    70→async def lifespan(application: FastAPI):
    71→    """Manage application lifecycle — initialize and clean up shared resources.
    72→
    73→    Resources stored on app.state are available via request.app.state in endpoints.
    74→    This replaces module-level singleton globals, eliminating:
    75→    - Test pollution (conftest resets no longer needed)
    76→    - Stale event loop errors (resources created in the running loop)
    77→    - Import-order dependencies (resources initialized at startup, not import time)
    78→    """
    79→    logger.info("Cohezion API starting up — initializing shared resources")

Good. Here's what the audit reveals and what I propose.

---

## Codebase Autopsy

| Metric | Value | Verdict |
|--------|-------|---------|
| Source files | 889 (.py) | Sprawling |
| Total source LOC | **158,251** | Massive |
| Tests collected | 7,226 | But... |
| Code coverage | **22%** | Brutal honesty |
| Lint errors | **631** (36 real bugs: undefined names) | Rotting |
| Files > 500 lines | **30+** (hard limit violation) | Structural debt |
| Files imported by nothing | **30+** totaling **18,779 lines** | Dead weight |
| Worst offender | `api/__init__.py` at **2,223 lines** | 7x hard limit |

## The Diagnosis

Cohezion has accumulated massive structural debt — **~19K lines of dead code**, 30+ god files, 36 real bugs (undefined names that would crash at runtime), and 22% coverage masking the fact that much of the codebase is untested ghost infrastructure. Every future session pays a tax navigating this.

## The Overnight Transformation: **Structural Purification**

Three phases, in order of impact:

### Phase 1: Dead Code Exorcism (~18,779 lines)
Remove the 30 source files that **nothing imports**. These are ghost modules — they inflate lint counts, confuse navigation, and add zero runtime value. Examples:
- `cost_aware_router_costawarerouter.py` (1,468 lines) — duplicated module name
- `model_pool_metrics.py` (960 lines), `batch_sizer_secure.py` (955 lines)
- `quantum_performance_monitor.py` (902 lines), `rollback.py` (888 lines)
- Plus ~25 more files, none imported by anything

### Phase 2: Bug Extermination (36 F821 + 75 F405)
Fix every `F821 Undefined name` error — these are **real runtime crashes waiting to happen** (undefined `logger`, missing `HardwareMetrics`, etc.). Then fix the `F405` star-import issues that mask more undefined names.

### Phase 3: God File Decomposition (Top 5)
Split the worst offenders into focused modules:
- `api/__init__.py` (2,223 lines) → routes, middleware, telemetry, models
- `routes_legacy.py` (1,529 lines) → domain-specific route modules
- `cohezion_mcp.py` (1,474 lines) → tool groups
- `executor.py` (1,148 lines) → pipeline stages
- `surreal_client.py` (1,098 lines) → query builders, connection, migrations

### Expected Outcome

| Before | After |
|--------|-------|
| 158,251 LOC | ~139,000 LOC (-12%) |
| 631 lint errors | ~200 (auto-fixable remainder) |
| 36 undefined name bugs | 0 |
| 30+ files > 500 lines | < 10 |
| 22% coverage | Same %, but denominator is smaller = meaningful |
| Navigation: haystack | Navigation: focused modules |

This is the foundation work that makes **every future session** faster, cleaner, and more productive. It's not glamorous — it's transcendent infrastructure.

Want me to start executing, or would you like to adjust the scope?

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.claude/skills/codebase-structural-purification/SKILL.md",
  "content": "---\nname: codebase-structural-purification\ndescription: |\n  Large-scale codebase dead code removal and lint bug elimination. Use when:\n  (1) codebase has accumulated ghost modules that inflate test counts/failures,\n  (2) lint errors include F821 undefined names (real runtime crashes),\n  (3) F405 star-import warnings masking undefined names,\n  (4) test suite shows more errors/failures than expected for working code,\n  (5) ruff --statistics shows >200 errors across a mature codebase.\n  Key insight: dead files only referenced by .md files are truly dead.\n  Removing them can eliminate hundreds of test errors and lint violations at once.\nauthor: Claude Code\nversion: 1.0.0\n---\n\n# Codebase Structural Purification\n\n## Problem\n\nOver time, large codebases accumulate:\n- Ghost modules: files that nothing imports (only referenced in .md index files)\n- F821 errors: undefined names that crash at runtime\n- F405/F403 errors: star imports masking undefined names\n- Orphaned tests: test files that test the dead modules\n\nThese compounds — dead code inflates lint counts, test collectors load ghost tests that error, and real bugs hide in the noise.\n\n## Key Insight: The Two-Pass Dead Code Check\n\nA single `grep -rl \"module_name\"` will flag files referenced only in `.md` index files as \"alive.\" **These are dead.** The correct check is:\n\n```bash\n# WRONG - counts .md references as \"alive\"\nrefs=$(grep -rl \"$module_name\" src/ tests/ scripts/ 2>/dev/null | grep -v \"$f\" | wc -l)\n\n# CORRECT - only count .py file references\npy_refs=$(grep -rl \"$module_name\" src/ tests/ scripts/ 2>/dev/null \\\n  | grep -v \"$f\" \\\n  | grep -v __pycache__ \\\n  | grep \"\\.py$\" \\\n  | wc -l)\n# If py_refs == 0, the file is dead regardless of .md references\n```\n\n## Cascade Effect\n\nRemoving dead source files produces a cascade:\n- Dead source → dead tests stop being collected → error count drops dramatically\n- In one session: 958 test errors → 23, 180 failures → 16, just from removing 68 dead files\n\n## Solution\n\n### Phase 1: Dead Code Verification\n\n```bash\n# Find ALL dead .py files (not imported by any other .py)\nfind src/ -name \"*.py\" -not -name \"__init__.py\" | while read f; do\n  module_name=$(basename \"$f\" .py)\n  py_refs=$(grep -rl \"$module_name\" src/ tests/ scripts/ 2>/dev/null \\\n    | grep -v \"$f\" | grep -v __pycache__ | grep \"\\.py$\" | wc -l)\n  if [ \"$py_refs\" -eq 0 ]; then\n    lines=$(wc -l < \"$f\")\n    [ \"$lines\" -gt 50 ] && echo \"$lines $f\"\n  fi\ndone | sort -rn\n```\n\n**Before deleting, verify**: single-ref files may be referenced only in `.md` docs. Check:\n```bash\ngrep -rl \"$module_name\" src/ tests/ scripts/ 2>/dev/null | grep -v \"$f\" | grep -v __pycache__\n```\nIf results are only `.md` files — the module is dead.\n\n### Phase 2: Safe Removal\n\n```bash\n# Remove confirmed dead files\nrm -v path/to/dead_file.py\n\n# Clear pycache to prevent stale bytecode\nfind src/ tests/ -type d -name __pycache__ -exec rm -rf {} + 2>/dev/null\n\n# Verify package still imports\nuv run python -c \"import your_package; print('OK')\"\n```\n\n### Phase 3: Fix F821/F405/F403 Errors\n\n**F821 → F405 relationship**: Star imports cause F405, which masks F821. Fix star imports first.\n\n```bash\n# See all undefined name errors\nuv run ruff check src/ --select F821 --output-format full\n\n# See star import issues\nuv run ruff check src/ --select F403,F405 --output-format full\n```\n\n**Fix star imports** (`from .models import *`) with explicit imports:\n```bash\n# Find all names actually used from the star import\nuv run ruff check path/to/file.py --select F405 --output-format concise | grep -oP '`\\K[^`]+' | sort -u\n```\nThen replace the star import with the explicit list.\n\n**Fix F821 patterns**:\n- `undefined name 'X'` in a string annotation → use `TYPE_CHECKING` guard\n- `undefined name 'logger'` → add `logging.getLogger(__name__)`\n- Variable typo in comprehension (`t for _, _, ts`) → rename to match\n\n```python\n# TYPE_CHECKING pattern for forward references\nfrom __future__ import annotations\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    from module import SomeClass\n\ndef method(self) -> \"SomeClass\":  # or just SomeClass with annotations future\n    ...\n```\n\n### Phase 4: Auto-Fix Remaining Lint\n\n```bash\n# Safe auto-fixes (never --unsafe-fixes per project rules)\nuv run ruff check src/ --fix\nuv run ruff format src/\n```\n\n### Phase 5: Verify\n\n```bash\nuv run pytest -q --tb=line 2>&1 | tail -5\nuv run ruff check src/ --statistics 2>&1 | tail -3\n```\n\n## Metrics to Expect\n\nOn a codebase with ~100K+ lines of accumulated ghost code:\n\n| Metric | Typical Improvement |\n|--------|-------------------|\n| Source LOC | -10 to -20% |\n| Lint errors | -60 to -75% |\n| Test failures | -80 to -95% |\n| Test errors | -90 to -98% |\n| F821 runtime bugs | -100% |\n\n## Verification\n\n```bash\n# Package still importable\nuv run python -c \"import package; print('OK')\"\n\n# Test suite not regressed (failures should only go DOWN)\nuv run pytest -q --tb=no 2>&1 | tail -3\n\n# Zero F821/F405/F403 errors\nuv run ruff check src/ --select F821,F405,F403 2>&1\n```\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- **Don't use `grep -r` alone** — it includes .md hits that make dead files look alive\n- **Don't remove `__init__.py` files** — even empty ones are needed for package discovery\n- **Check `scripts/` and config files too** — some modules are referenced in shell scripts\n- **`__main__.py` may be dead in import graph but is an entry point** — verify via pyproject.toml `[project.scripts]`\n- **Never use `--unsafe-fixes`** — TC001/TC002/TC003 moves imports to TYPE_CHECKING, breaking `patch()` calls in tests\n"
}

     1→# KEY LEARNINGS
     2→
     3→## VLIW & Low-Level Optimization (Learnings 1-11, summarized)
     4→
     5→Packet-greedy scheduling + register windowing + SIMD vectorization achieved 423x speedup (349 cycles) on Anthropic's VLIW challenge. Key insights: barrier-locked manifolds prevent temporal instruction leakage; batch processing inside Rust (via rayon) amortizes FFI overhead for 29x speedup over naive 1:1 calls; windowing provides the largest single performance jump after SIMD.
     6→
     7→---
     8→
     9→## Learning 12: Kineticization of the 12D Manifold (2026-02-05)
    10→
    11→A 12D manifold must be grounded in the physical substrate (CPU pressure, VRAM density, dilation factor, RAM-weighted semantic intent) to avoid being a purely semantic "Potemkin Universe."
    12→
    13→## Learning 13: VLIW-to-Cognition Abstraction (2026-02-05)
    14→
    15→VLIW architecture parallels biological reasoning — processing 2048D vectors as "instruction packets" for deterministic, slot-based execution of thought. Implemented in `flume_physics.rs`.
    16→
    17→## Learning 14: The Organic Modularity Axiom
    18→
    19→Aesthetically bridging high-performance silicon heritage with ecological branding. "Inspired Motifs" maintain legal sovereignty while honoring lineage, increasing user trust.
    20→
    21→## Learning 15: The Peaked Manifold Approximation
    22→
    23→In peaked quantum circuits, state compresses to a low-rank MPS (Bond 64-256) without losing the signal. Manual SWAP routing maintains 1D topology; eager SVD contraction prevents tensor network explosion. 16x bond reduction → 100x throughput with 1e-5 vs 1e-11 signal separation.
    24→
    25→## Learning 16: VLIW Latent Alignment & Temporal Stability
    26→
    27→Instruction stability in VLIW is a latent manifold problem. Barrier-locked manifolds + VLEN=8 alignment ensure hardware cache coherence.
    28→
    29→## Learning 17: Subagent Delegation Topology
    30→
    31→Hierarchical agent topology (Scout/Strategist) outperforms monolithic models. Scouts (Qwen-Coder 30b) do high-speed sensing; Strategists (DeepSeek-R1 70b) do deep reasoning.
    32→
    33→## Learning 18: Biological Recursion in Silico
    34→
    35→Stability through mortality — introducing apoptosis and mitosis forces dynamic equilibrium (HIHO state). Immortal agents stagnate.
    36→
    37→## Learning 19: The Specialist Roster Effectiveness
    38→
    39→Cognitive specialization > parameters. Routed swarm of domain experts (DeepSeek-R1-8B, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B, Phi4-Mini) outperforms generic 7B model.
    40→
    41→## Learning 20: VRAM Persistence & The Sudo Trap
    42→
    43→Automated recovery must never rely on `sudo`. Use direct Ollama `/api/generate` with `keep_alive: 0` and AMD `/sys` telemetry for non-privileged VRAM management.
    44→
    45→## Learning 22: Agentic Reasoning Paradigms
    46→
    47→Refinement over generation — agentic reasoning shifts from next-token prediction to state-machine planning with recursive verification, checkpointing, and Merkle indexing.
    48→
    49→## Learning 26: The Python Autoregression Bottleneck
    50→
    51→GIL limits autoregressive decoding to ~10Hz. Inference loops must move to compiled languages (Rust/C++) for 100Hz+ fluid behavior.
    52→
    53→## Learning 27: Rust FFI Bridge Success
    54→
    55→PyO3 + maturin + uv provides seamless Rust-Python bridge. Critical: ensure LD_LIBRARY_PATH/PYTHONPATH for shared object linking during testing.
    56→
    57→## Learning 28: FFI Overhead & The Batching Pivot
    58→
    59→Naive 1:1 FFI calls = 0.2x speedup (regression). Moving iteration inside Rust with rayon = 29.1x speedup (20.45s → 0.70s for 10k items).

     1→# SKILL: REPOSITORY_HEALTH_PRIME
     2→
     3→## DOMAIN EXPERTISE
     4→You are a repository health engineer specializing in preventing and detecting repository bloat. You implement the **3-Layer Governance** pattern: Prevention (pre-commit hooks), Detection (automated monitoring), and Remediation (cleanup procedures). You ensure repositories stay lean (<6GB), maintainable, and fast.
     5→
     6→## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS
     7→- **Repository Bloat**: Accumulation of unnecessary data in .git directory causing slow operations and excessive disk usage.
     8→- **3-Layer Governance**:
     9→  1. Prevention: Pre-commit hooks block files >1MB before they enter history
    10→  2. Detection: CI/CD monitors repository size, large files, pack efficiency
    11→  3. Remediation: Documented procedures for cleanup (git gc, filter-repo, lfs migration)
    12→- **Pack Efficiency**: Git's internal compression ratio. Low efficiency (many loose objects) indicates need for `git gc`.
    13→- **Size Thresholds**:
    14→  - **Target**: <6GB for optimal performance
    15→  - **Warning**: 6-10GB approaching limit, consider cleanup
    16→  - **Critical**: >10GB requires immediate remediation
    17→- **Large File Patterns**: Binary files (*.pt, *.pth, *.ckpt), logs, checkpoints that should use git-lfs or .gitignore
    18→
    19→## INSTRUCTION
    20→
    21→### 1. Assessment Phase
    22→Run comprehensive repository health scan:
    23→
    24→```bash
    25→# Check repository size
    26→du -sh .git
    27→SIZE_BYTES=$(du -sb .git | cut -f1)
    28→SIZE_GB=$((SIZE_BYTES / 1024 / 1024 / 1024))
    29→
    30→# Find large files in history (>1MB)
    31→git rev-list --objects --all | \
    32→git cat-file --batch-check='%(objecttype) %(objectname) %(objectsize) %(rest)' | \
    33→awk '$1 == "blob" && $3 > 1048576 {printf "%.2f MB: %s\n", $3/1048576, $4}' | \
    34→sort -rn | head -20
    35→
    36→# Check pack efficiency
    37→git count-objects -vH
    38→
    39→# Validate .gitignore coverage
    40→grep -E '^\*\.(pt|pth|ckpt|bin|log)$|^(logs|checkpoints|tmp|\.cache)/' .gitignore
    41→```
    42→
    43→**Health Criteria**:
    44→- ✅ HEALTHY: <6GB, <20 large files, good pack efficiency
    45→- ⚠️  WARNING: 6-10GB, 20-50 large files, moderate pack issues
    46→- ❌ CRITICAL: >10GB, >50 large files, poor pack efficiency
    47→
    48→### 2. Prevention Layer (Pre-Commit Hooks)
    49→Ensure `.pre-commit-config.yaml` includes:
    50→
    51→```yaml
    52→- repo: https://github.com/pre-commit/pre-commit-hooks
    53→  rev: v5.0.0
    54→  hooks:
    55→    - id: check-added-large-files
    56→      args: [--maxkb=1000]  # Block files >1MB
    57→      stages: [pre-commit]
    58→    - id: detect-private-key
    59→      stages: [pre-commit]
    60→```
    61→
    62→**Verification**:
    63→```bash
    64→# Install hooks
    65→pre-commit install
    66→
    67→# Test blocking (should fail)
    68→dd if=/dev/zero of=test-large-file.bin bs=1M count=2
    69→git add test-large-file.bin
    70→git commit -m "test"  # Should be blocked
    71→rm test-large-file.bin
    72→```
    73→
    74→### 3. Detection Layer (CI/CD Monitoring)
    75→Deploy `.github/workflows/repo-health.yml`:
    76→
    77→**Triggers**:
    78→- On push to main/develop
    79→- On pull request to main
    80→- Weekly schedule (Sunday 2am UTC)
    81→- Manual dispatch
    82→
    83→**Checks**:
    84→1. Repository size (fail if >10GB, warn if >6GB)
    85→2. Large files scan (warn if >50 files >1MB)
    86→3. Pack efficiency measurement
    87→4. .gitignore coverage validation
    88→
    89→**Alerts**:
    90→- GitHub Actions errors/warnings
    91→- Artifacts uploaded with large file list
    92→- Health summary in workflow logs
    93→
    94→### 4. Remediation Procedures
    95→
    96→#### Quick Cleanup (<5 min)
    97→For repositories 6-10GB with good pack efficiency:
    98→
    99→```bash
   100→# Aggressive garbage collection
   101→git gc --aggressive --prune=now
   102→
   103→# Repack objects
   104→git repack -Ad
   105→
   106→# Verify reduction
   107→du -sh .git
   108→```
   109→
   110→**Expected**: 20-50% size reduction
   111→
   112→#### Medium Cleanup (30-60 min)
   113→For repositories >10GB or >50 large files:
   114→
   115→```bash
   116→# Identify largest files
   117→git rev-list --objects --all | \
   118→git cat-file --batch-check='%(objecttype) %(objectname) %(objectsize) %(rest)' | \
   119→awk '$1 == "blob"' | sort -k3 -rn | head -20 > large_files.txt
   120→
   121→# Option A: Migrate to git-lfs
   122→git lfs install
   123→git lfs track "*.pt" "*.pth" "*.ckpt"
   124→git add .gitattributes
   125→git commit -m "chore: Configure git-lfs for large model files"
   126→
   127→# Option B: Remove from history (DESTRUCTIVE)
   128→# Create backup first!
   129→git tag backup-pre-cleanup
   130→git filter-repo --path-glob '*.pt' --invert-paths --force
   131→```
   132→
   133→**Warning**: History rewriting requires force-push and team coordination.
   134→
   135→#### Deep Cleanup (2-4 hours)
   136→For severely bloated repositories or when filter-repo stalls:
   137→
   138→```bash
   139→# 1. Create backup
   140→git clone --mirror . ../cohezion-backup.git
   141→
   142→# 2. Identify bloat sources
   143→git rev-list --all --objects | \
   144→  git cat-file --batch-check | \
   145→  awk '{print $1, $3, $2}' | \
   146→  sort -k2 -rn | head -100 > bloat_analysis.txt
   147→
   148→# 3. Remove large directories from history
   149→git filter-repo --path src/cohezion/knowledge_graph/universe_nodes/linguistic_evolution/logs/ \
   150→  --invert-paths --force
   151→
   152→# 4. Aggressive cleanup
   153→git gc --aggressive --prune=now
   154→git repack -Ad
   155→
   156→# 5. Verify and sync
   157→du -sh .git
   158→git remote add origin-new <url>
   159→git push origin-new --all --force
   160→git push origin-new --tags --force
   161→```
   162→
   163→**Fallback**: If filter-repo hangs (>4 hours), use git gc approach instead.
   164→
   165→### 5. Governance Maintenance
   166→
   167→#### Weekly Tasks (Automated via Cron)
   168→```bash
   169→# Sunday 2am UTC - scripts/maintenance/weekly_repo_maintenance.sh
   170→#!/bin/bash
   171→set -euo pipefail
   172→
   173→# Check repository health
   174→SIZE_GB=$(du -sb .git | awk '{print $1/1024/1024/1024}')
   175→
   176→if (( $(echo "$SIZE_GB > 6" | bc -l) )); then
   177→  echo "⚠️  Repository size: ${SIZE_GB}GB (target: <6GB)"
   178→  git gc --auto
   179→fi
   180→
   181→# Pack efficiency check
   182→git count-objects -vH
   183→
   184→# Large file count
   185→LARGE_FILE_COUNT=$(git rev-list --objects --all | \
   186→  git cat-file --batch-check='%(objecttype) %(objectname) %(objectsize) %(rest)' | \
   187→  awk '$1 == "blob" && $3 > 1048576' | wc -l)
   188→
   189→echo "Large files (>1MB): $LARGE_FILE_COUNT"
   190→
   191→# Alert if thresholds exceeded
   192→if [ "$LARGE_FILE_COUNT" -gt 50 ]; then
   193→  echo "::warning::High large file count ($LARGE_FILE_COUNT)"
   194→fi
   195→```
   196→
   197→#### Monthly Reviews
   198→1. **Size Trend Analysis**: Compare month-over-month growth
   199→2. **Large File Audit**: Review files >1MB, determine if needed
   200→3. **Pack Efficiency**: Ensure <10% loose objects
   201→4. **Hook Compliance**: Verify all developers have hooks installed
   202→
   203→## SUCCESS CRITERIA
   204→- ✅ Repository size <6GB (target) or <10GB (limit)
   205→- ✅ Pre-commit hooks block files >1MB
   206→- ✅ CI/CD workflow runs weekly and alerts on violations
   207→- ✅ Cleanup procedures documented and tested
   208→- ✅ Team trained on governance practices
   209→
   210→## CHARTER ALIGNMENT
   211→This skill aligns with:
   212→- **Charter**: "Deterministic Responsibility" - Proactive health monitoring prevents unpredictable repository failures
   213→- **Constitution**: "Honesty" - Transparent size metrics and thresholds
   214→- **HIHO Stability**: Maintain repository in stable state (0.5 coherence = optimal size range)
   215→
   216→## METRICS
   217→Track in SurrealDB:

   223→  pack_efficiency: $pack_efficiency,

   225→  hiho_stable: ($size_gb >= 4 AND $size_gb <= 8)  -- 6GB ± 2GB = HIHO range
   226→};
   227→```
   228→
   229→## EXAMPLES

   234→5.9G    .git
   235→

   239→in-pack: 234567
   240→packs: 1

   245→
   246→### Example 2: Warning State

   253→

   257→
   258→### Example 3: Critical State

   268→#   1. Create backup tag

   272→
   273→## ERROR HANDLING
   274→
   275→### Hook Installation Failures

   278→# Solution: Check Python environment
   279→uv run pre-commit install
   280→
   281→# Verify hooks

   283→```
   284→
   285→### CI/CD Workflow Not Running
   286→```bash
   287→# Symptom: repo-health.yml not executing
   288→# Solution: Check workflow permissions
   289→# GitHub Settings → Actions → Workflow permissions → Read and write
   290→```
   291→
   292→### Git GC Hangs/Stalls
   293→```bash
   294→# Symptom: git gc --aggressive runs >4 hours
   295→# Solution: Kill process, use faster method
   296→kill <pid>
   297→git repack -Ad
   298→git prune
   299→```
   300→
   301→### Filter-Repo Corrupts Repository

   306→cd cohezion-restored
   307→git remote add origin <url>
   308→```

```
## Learning 139: pyproject.toml Curated Ignore List for ML/Simulation Codebases (2026-02-23)

ML/simulation codebases trigger many ruff security rules as false positives. Better to add a project-level ignore list than scatter thousands of per-line `# noqa` comments. Categories for Cohezion: `S311` (random for simulation, not security), `S603/S607` (subprocess in infrastructure scripts, legitimate), `N806` (uppercase variables X/Y/Z are conventional in physics/ML code), `S108` (temp files in test helpers, not production), `S608` (SurrealDB queries are parameterized — not traditional SQL injection). Process: (1) run `ruff check src/ --statistics` to identify false-positive categories by count, (2) verify each category is genuinely non-security before ignoring globally, (3) set line-length to 120 for scientific code with long type hints. Result: 831 → 203 errors (75.6% reduction) without losing meaningful coverage.

## Learning 142: Diffusion-Based Reasoning (dLLM) Speed Multiplier (2026-03-04)

Traditional autoregressive models process tokens sequentially, creating a hard latency floor for multi-step agentic reasoning. Integrating Inception Labs' Mercury-2 diffusion model (dLLM) replaced sequential generation with parallel refinement, yielding a 22.6x speedup (1.48s vs 33.47s per turn) in our benchmarks. This fundamentally shifts the bottleneck of AI orchestration from inference speed to application logic, making complex swarms viable in real-time.

## Learning 143: The Ouroboros Self-Correction Kernel (2026-03-04)

High-throughput reasoning (1,000+ tps) enables the "Ouroboros" pattern: wrapping generation in an autonomous, high-frequency Generate-Critic-Refine loop. Because a complete 3-turn Ouroboros cycle on a dLLM (4.4s) is significantly faster than a single autoregressive turn (33s), we can intentionally trade this excess speed for extreme reliability. The system self-corrects logic and security drift in real-time without violating user latency expectations.

## Learning 144: Gateway Architecture and Non-Blocking I/O (2026-03-04)

When building high-throughput provider gateways (e.g., `InceptionGateway`, `NgrokAIGateway`), utilizing blocking I/O (like the standard `requests` library) inside `async` generator methods causes immediate event loop starvation under load, neutralizing any speed gains from the model. Moving to `httpx.AsyncClient` combined with a unified `ModelRegistry` for centralized cost mapping ensures the routing layer scales linearly and accurately tracks finances across diverse providers.

## Learning 145: Autonomous Marathon Bleed and Pruning (2026-03-04)

Autonomous skill enrichment loops ("Red Wall Marathons") generate massive amounts of code, tests (e.g., `shadowscripter`), and documentation modifications. Left unchecked, a single session can produce 11,000+ pending changes across 900+ files. **Rule of Autonomous Execution:** Unsupervised loops must never commit directly to the `main` or release branches, and their working state must be treated as ephemeral telemetry. Protocol: (1) Capture telemetry and phi_scores, (2) Synthesize the learnings, (3) Prune the workspace with a hard reset (`git reset --hard` & `git clean -fd`). This prevents experimental "shadow" tests and active padding mutations from destabilizing the core repository.
```

This prevents experimental \"shadow\" tests and active padding mutations from destabilizing the core repository.\n\n## Learning 146: Python Source Structural Rot and the .md-Index False Positive (2026-03-05)\n\nCohezion accumulated 68 ghost Python modules (26,746 lines) that nothing imported — each only referenced in `src/cohezion/INDEX.md`. A naive `grep -rl \"module_name\"` returns 1 hit for these, making them appear alive. The correct dead-code check filters to `.py`-only references: `grep -rl \"$name\" src/ tests/ | grep \"\\.py$\"`. The errors weren't logic bugs; they were import failures against modules that no longer existed in any meaningful sense.\n\n**F821 → F405 relationship:** `F405` (star-import undefined) *masks* `F821` (truly undefined name). Fix `from .module import *` with explicit imports first; the real undefined names become visible. In routes_legacy.py, eliminating one star import exposed 0 new F821 errors (already masked correctly) and cleaned 75 F405 violations.\n\n**Rule of Source Hygiene:** Run `find src/ -name \"*.py\" | xargs grep -l \".\"` is insufficient — use import-graph reachability. A file untouched by any `.py` import is structurally dead regardless of file size, documentation coverage, or apparent completeness. See `PYTHON_SOURCE_HEALTH_PRIME.md`."

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/skills/PYTHON_SOURCE_HEALTH_PRIME.md",
  "content": "# SKILL: PYTHON_SOURCE_HEALTH_PRIME\n\n## DOMAIN EXPERTISE\nPython source-code structural health. Detecting and eliminating ghost modules (dead code reachable only via `.md` index files), cascading lint errors (F821/F405 chains), and star-import blindness — distinct from git repository bloat (see `REPOSITORY_HEALTH_PRIME`) and git index ghost files (see `REPO_HYGIENE_PRIME`).\n\n## KEY TEXTS & CONCEPTS\n\n- **Ghost Module**: A `.py` file that no other `.py` file imports. May appear alive because `INDEX.md` or architecture docs reference it by name. Import-graph reachability is the only reliable test.\n- **The .md-Index False Positive**: `grep -rl \"module_name\"` returns 1 hit for a ghost module referenced only in `INDEX.md`. Must filter to `.py` references only.\n- **Error Cascade**: Ghost modules carry ghost tests. Removing dead source silently eliminates import-error test failures. 68 modules removed → 935 test errors eliminated without touching tests.\n- **F821 → F405 Masking Chain**: Star imports (`from .models import *`) produce `F405` (may be undefined) which hides `F821` (truly undefined). Fix star imports first; F821 become visible.\n- **HIHO Stability via Source Entropy**: A codebase where 17% of lines are unreachable is in structural HIHO instability. Dead code raises apparent test failures, inflates lint counts, and poisons coherence metrics.\n\n## THRESHOLDS\n\n| Signal | Healthy | Warning | Critical |\n|--------|---------|---------|---------|\n| Ghost modules (`.py` files with 0 `.py` importers) | 0 | 1–10 | >10 |\n| F821 undefined name errors | 0 | 1–5 | >5 |\n| F403/F405 star-import errors | 0 | 1–10 | >10 |\n| Source LOC attributable to dead code | <1% | 1–5% | >5% |\n| Test errors from import failures | 0 | 1–20 | >20 |\n\n## DETECTION PROTOCOL\n\n### Step 1: Import-Graph Dead Code Scan\n```bash\nfind src/ -name \"*.py\" -not -name \"__init__.py\" | while read f; do\n  module=$(basename \"$f\" .py)\n  py_refs=$(grep -rl \"$module\" src/ tests/ scripts/ 2>/dev/null \\\n    | grep -v \"$f\" | grep -v __pycache__ | grep \"\\.py$\" | wc -l)\n  if [ \"$py_refs\" -eq 0 ]; then\n    lines=$(wc -l < \"$f\")\n    [ \"$lines\" -gt 50 ] && echo \"$lines $f\"\n  fi\ndone | sort -rn\n```\nFiles with 0 `.py` importers are ghost modules regardless of size or documentation.\n\n### Step 2: Verify False Positives\nSingle-ref files may be aliased, dynamically loaded, or entry points:\n```bash\n# Check what the one reference is\ngrep -rl \"$module\" src/ tests/ scripts/ *.toml 2>/dev/null | grep -v \"$f\"\n# If only .md files → dead. If pyproject.toml scripts section → entry point, keep.\n```\n\n### Step 3: Lint Fingerprint\n```bash\nuv run ruff check src/ --select F821,F403,F405,F401 --statistics\n```\n- `F821 > 0` → runtime crash waiting to happen\n- `F403 > 0` → star import masking other names; fix before F821 can be trusted\n- `F401 > 10` → indicator of dead code accumulation\n\n## REMEDIATION PROTOCOL\n\n### Remove Ghost Modules\n```bash\nrm -v path/to/ghost_module.py\nfind src/ tests/ -type d -name __pycache__ -exec rm -rf {} + 2>/dev/null\nuv run python -c \"import cohezion; print('OK')\"  # Verify package intact\n```\n\n### Fix F821 Undefined Names\nPattern-match by error type:\n```python\n# Missing logger → add at top of file\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# Forward reference to conditionally imported class → TYPE_CHECKING guard\nfrom __future__ import annotations\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    from module import ClassName\n\n# Variable name collision in comprehension ([t for _, _, ts in ...])\n# → rename the unused variable to match its usage\n```\n\n### Eliminate Star Imports\n```bash\n# Get all names actually used from the star import\nuv run ruff check path/to/file.py --select F405 --output-format concise \\\n  | grep -oP '`\\K[^`]+' | sort -u\n```\nReplace `from .models import *` with the explicit list. Include any non-model names\n(logger, helper functions) that came from parent module's namespace.\n\n### Auto-Fix Remaining\n```bash\nuv run ruff check src/ --fix          # Safe auto-fixes only\nuv run ruff format src/               # Format\n# NEVER --unsafe-fixes: moves imports to TYPE_CHECKING, breaks patch() in tests\n```\n\n## SUCCESS CRITERIA\n- ✅ Zero ghost modules with >50 lines\n- ✅ Zero F821 undefined name errors\n- ✅ Zero F403/F405 star-import errors\n- ✅ Test error count < 50 (pre-existing integration failures only)\n- ✅ `uv run python -c \"import cohezion\"` succeeds\n\n## FUTURE HOOKS\n1. **Compound Loop Integration**: Run detection scan as pre-execution health check in `CompoundExecutor` — abort if F821 count > 0 (runtime crashes corrupt execution trajectories)\n2. **DegradationDetector Hook**: Add `source_entropy` dimension to the 12D coherence vector — ghost module ratio as a coherence component\n3. **Automated Sentinel**: `GitSentinel` (see REPO_HYGIENE_PRIME) should also flag: new `.py` files with 0 importers after a session, treating them as structural debt before they accumulate\n4. **CI/CD Gate**: Add `uv run ruff check src/ --select F821 --exit-zero` check to CI — fail PR if new F821 errors introduced\n\n## COMPOUND IMPACT\n- Removing 68 ghost modules eliminated 26,746 lines of dead code (-17% total LOC)\n- Test error cascade: 958 → 23 errors (-98%) without touching any test\n- Lint noise reduction: 631 → 198 errors (-69%)\n- Real bug density increased (fewer false positives masking real issues)\n\n## VERSION\nv1.0 (2026-03-05)\n\n## SEE ALSO\n- `REPOSITORY_HEALTH_PRIME` (git binary/object bloat — different axis)\n- `REPO_HYGIENE_PRIME` (git index ghost files — different axis)\n- `TESTING_PRIME` (test isolation and singleton pollution)\n- `Learning 139` (pyproject.toml ML false-positive ignore list)\n- `Learning 146` (the .md-index false positive discovery)\n"
}

A codebase with 17% dead code has coherence ~0.83 on this axis\n3. **Automated CI gate**: New F821 errors on a PR = merge block. Dead code accumulates because there's no automatic detection at the commit boundary\n\n## Pitfalls"
}

    18→from pydantic import BaseModel

    25→
    26→logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)
    27→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    28→
    29→# Allowed CORS origins from environment, default to localhost only
    30→_CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get("COHEZION_CORS_ORIGINS", "http://localhost:3000,http://localhost:8080").split(",")
    31→
    32→app = FastAPI(
    33→    title="Cohezion API",
    34→    description="AI Research Lab API - Swarm workflows and MCP tools",
    35→    version="0.1.0",
    36→    docs_url="/docs" if os.environ.get("COHEZION_ENV") != "production" else None,
    37→    redoc_url="/redoc" if os.environ.get("COHEZION_ENV") != "production" else None,
    38→)
    39→
    40→# CORS — restricted to configured origins with explicit methods/headers
    41→app.add_middleware(
    42→    CORSMiddleware,
    43→    allow_origins=_CORS_ORIGINS,
    44→    allow_credentials=False,
    45→    allow_methods=["GET", "POST"],
    46→    allow_headers=["Content-Type", "Authorization", "X-Agent-Token"],
    47→)
    48→
    49→
    50→# Rate limiting middleware
    51→@app.middleware("http")
    52→async def rate_limit_middleware(request: Request, call_next):
    53→    limiter = get_rate_limiter()
    54→    client_ip = request.client.host if request.client else "unknown"
    55→    result = limiter.check(client_ip, request.url.path)
    56→    if not result.allowed:
    57→        return JSONResponse(
    58→            status_code=429,
    59→            headers={
    60→                "Retry-After": str(int(result.reset_after) + 1),
    61→                "X-RateLimit-Limit": str(result.limit),
    62→                "X-RateLimit-Remaining": "0",
    63→            },
    64→            content={"detail": "Rate limit exceeded"},
    65→        )
    66→    response = await call_next(request)
    67→    response.headers["X-RateLimit-Remaining"] = str(result.remaining)
    68→    return response
    69→
    70→
    71→# Static files
    72→static_dir = Path(__file__).parent / "static"
    73→if static_dir.exists():
    74→    app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory=static_dir, html=True), name="static")
    75→
    76→
    77→# Root redirect to UI
    78→@app.get("/")
    79→async def root():
    80→    return RedirectResponse(url="/static/index.html")

   197→
   198→    # Validate name: only allow alphanumeric, dash, underscore (prevent path traversal)
   199→    if not re.match(r"^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$", name):
   200→        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid notebook name")
   201→
   202→    base_dir = Path("docs/notebooks").resolve()
   203→    notebook_path = (base_dir / f"{name}.md").resolve()
   204→
   205→    # Ensure resolved path stays within the base directory
   206→    if not str(notebook_path).startswith(str(base_dir)):
   207→        raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail="Access denied")
   208→
   209→    if not notebook_path.exists():
   210→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Notebook not found")
   211→    return {"name": name, "content": notebook_path.read_text()}
   212→
   213→
   214→# Simulation endpoints
   215→@app.get("/simulations")
   216→async def list_simulations():
   217→    """List all physics simulations."""
   218→    import json
   219→    from pathlib import Path
   220→
   221→    sim_file = Path("src/cohezion/knowledge_graph/universe_nodes/physics_simulations.json")
   222→    if not sim_file.exists():
   223→        return {"simulations": []}
   224→    data = json.loads(sim_file.read_text())
   225→    return {"simulations": [s["id"] for s in data.get("simulations", [])]}
   226→
   227→
   228→@app.get("/simulations/{sim_id}")
   229→async def get_simulation(sim_id: str):
   230→    """Get a specific simulation result."""
   231→    import json
   232→    from pathlib import Path
   233→
   234→    sim_file = Path("src/cohezion/knowledge_graph/universe_nodes/physics_simulations.json")
   235→    if not sim_file.exists():
   236→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="No simulations found")
   237→    data = json.loads(sim_file.read_text())
   238→    for sim in data.get("simulations", []):
   239→        if sim["id"] == sim_id:
   240→            return sim
   241→    raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Simulation {sim_id} not found")
   242→
   243→
   244→# Journey endpoints - Agent trajectory visualization
   245→@app.get("/journeys")
   246→async def list_journeys():
   247→    """List recent agent journeys."""
   248→    from cohezion.swarm.journey_tracker import get_journey_tracker
   249→
   250→    tracker = get_journey_tracker()
   251→    journeys = tracker.get_recent_journeys(limit=20)
   252→    return {"journeys": [{"id": j["journey_id"], "query": j["query"][:50], "steps": j["step_count"]} for j in journeys]}
   253→
   254→
   255→@app.get("/journeys/{journey_id}")
   256→async def get_journey(journey_id: str):

   259→

   261→    journey_file = tracker.output_dir / f"{journey_id}.json"
   262→    if not journey_file.exists():
   263→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Journey {journey_id} not found")
   264→    import json
   265→
   266→    return json.loads(journey_file.read_text())
   267→
   268→
   269→@app.get("/journeys/{journey_id}/trajectory")
   270→async def get_journey_trajectory(journey_id: str):
   271→    """Get physics trajectory for visualization."""
   272→    from cohezion.swarm.journey_tracker import get_journey_tracker

   274→    tracker = get_journey_tracker()

   276→    if not trajectory:
   277→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Journey {journey_id} not found")
   278→    return {"trajectory": trajectory}
   279→

   414→@app.get("/journeys/{journey_id}/visualize")
   415→async def visualize_journey(journey_id: str):
   416→    """Render an animated visualization of the journey trajectory."""
   417→    import json
   418→    from pathlib import Path
   419→
   420→    import numpy as np
   421→    from fastapi.responses import FileResponse
   422→
   423→    from cohezion.swarm.journey_tracker import get_journey_tracker
   424→    from cohezion.viz.hypertools_renderer import HyperToolsViz
   425→
   426→    tracker = get_journey_tracker()
   427→    journey_file = tracker.output_dir / f"{journey_id}.json"
   428→
   429→    if not journey_file.exists():
   430→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Journey {journey_id} not found")
   431→
   432→    journey = json.loads(journey_file.read_text())
   433→
   434→    # Extract physics trajectory as numpy array
   435→    trajectory_data = []
   436→    for step in journey.get("steps", []):
   437→        ps = step.get("physics_state", {})
   438→        # Create 6D vector from physics state
   439→        vec = [
   440→            ps.get("x", 0),
   441→            ps.get("y", 0),
   442→            ps.get("z", 0),
   443→            ps.get("mass", 0.5),
   444→            ps.get("coherence", 0.5),
   445→            ps.get("novelty", 0.5),
   446→        ]
   447→        trajectory_data.append(vec)
   448→
   449→    if len(trajectory_data) < 2:
   450→        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Journey needs at least 2 steps for visualization")
   451→
   452→    trajectory = np.array(trajectory_data)
   453→
   454→    # Render with HyperTools
   455→    viz = HyperToolsViz(output_dir=Path("renders"))
   456→    output_path = viz.animate_trajectory(
   457→        trajectory,
   458→        output_name=f"journey_{journey_id}",
   459→        fps=2,  # Slow for visibility
   460→    )
   461→
   462→    return FileResponse(
   463→        output_path,

   465→        filename=f"{journey_id}_trajectory.gif",

   792→@app.post("/flume/train", response_model=FlumeTrainResponse)
   793→async def train_flume(request: FlumeTrainRequest):
   794→    """Trigger FLUME VAE training on synthetic data."""
   795→    from cohezion.flume.dataset import SyntheticFlumeDataset
   796→    from cohezion.flume.training import FlumeVAETrainer, TrainConfig
   797→
   798→    config = TrainConfig(
   799→        z_dim=request.z_dim,
   800→        batch_size=request.batch_size,
   801→        epochs=request.epochs,
   802→        lr=request.lr,
   803→        kl_weight=request.kl_weight,
   804→        coherence_weight=request.coherence_weight,
   805→    )
   806→
   807→    dataset = SyntheticFlumeDataset(n_samples=request.n_samples, z_dim=request.z_dim)
   808→    trainer = FlumeVAETrainer(config)
   809→
   810→    try:
   811→        metrics = trainer.train(dataset=dataset)
   812→    except Exception as e:
   813→        logger.error(f"FLUME training failed: {e}", exc_info=True)
   814→        raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail="Training failed") from e
   815→
   816→    final = metrics[-1]
   817→    checkpoint_dir = Path(config.checkpoint_dir)
   818→    ckpt_files = sorted(checkpoint_dir.glob("flume_vae_ep*.pt"))
   819→    checkpoint_path = str(ckpt_files[-1]) if ckpt_files else ""
   820→
   821→    return FlumeTrainResponse(
   822→        epochs_completed=len(metrics),
   823→        final_mse=final["mse"],
   824→        final_kl=final["kl"],
   825→        final_total=final["total"],
   826→        checkpoint_path=checkpoint_path,
   827→    )
   828→
   829→
   830→@app.get("/flume/status", response_model=FlumeStatusResponse)
   831→async def flume_status():
   832→    """Check FLUME VAE training status and latest checkpoint."""
   833→    checkpoint_dir = Path("data/flume/checkpoints")
   834→    if not checkpoint_dir.exists():
   835→        return FlumeStatusResponse(trained=False)
   836→
   837→    ckpt_files = sorted(checkpoint_dir.glob("flume_vae_ep*.pt"))
   838→    if not ckpt_files:
   839→        return FlumeStatusResponse(trained=False)
   840→
   841→    latest = ckpt_files[-1]
   842→
   843→    # Try to load metrics
   844→    metrics_file = checkpoint_dir / "training_metrics.json"
   845→    last_metrics = None
   846→    if metrics_file.exists():
   847→        import json
   848→
   849→        try:
   850→            all_metrics = json.loads(metrics_file.read_text())
   851→            if all_metrics:
   852→                last_metrics = all_metrics[-1] if isinstance(all_metrics, list) else all_metrics
   853→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError):
   854→            pass
   855→
   856→    return FlumeStatusResponse(
   857→        trained=True,
   858→        checkpoint_path=str(latest),
   859→        last_metrics=last_metrics,
   860→    )
   861→
   862→
   863→_vae_trainer = None
   864→
   865→
   866→def _get_vae():
   867→    """Lazy-load the trained FLUME VAE (singleton)."""
   868→    global _vae_trainer
   869→    if _vae_trainer is None:
   870→        import torch
   871→
   872→        from cohezion.flume.training import FlumeVAETrainer
   873→
   874→        _vae_trainer = FlumeVAETrainer()
   875→        ckpt_path = Path("data/flume/checkpoints/flume_vae_ep50.pt")
   876→        if ckpt_path.exists():
   877→            try:
   878→                ckpt = torch.load(ckpt_path, weights_only=True)
   879→                _vae_trainer.encoder.load_state_dict(ckpt["encoder"])
   880→                _vae_trainer.mu_head.load_state_dict(ckpt["mu_head"])
   881→                _vae_trainer.logvar_head.load_state_dict(ckpt["logvar_head"])
   882→                _vae_trainer.decoder.load_state_dict(ckpt["decoder"])
   883→                logger.info("Loaded FLUME VAE checkpoint: %s", ckpt_path)
   884→            except (RuntimeError, KeyError) as e:
   885→                logger.warning(
   886→                    "Failed to load FLUME VAE checkpoint %s (architecture mismatch?); using random weights: %s",
   887→                    ckpt_path,
   888→                    str(e),
   889→                )
   890→        else:
   891→            logger.warning("No FLUME VAE checkpoint found at %s; using random weights", ckpt_path)
   892→    return _vae_trainer
   893→
   894→

   894→
   895→def _compute_coherence(z: list[float], z_dim: int = 256) -> float:
   896→    """Compute HIHO coherence: 1.0 at mean=0.5, decays with variance."""
   897→    import numpy as np
   898→
   899→    arr = np.array(z)
   900→    n_chunks = min(12, z_dim)
   901→    chunk_size = z_dim // n_chunks
   902→    variance_sum = 0.0
   903→
   904→    for c in range(n_chunks):
   905→        start = c * chunk_size
   906→        end = (c + 1) * chunk_size if c < n_chunks - 1 else z_dim
   907→        chunk_mean = float(np.mean(arr[start:end]))
   908→        variance_sum += (chunk_mean - 0.5) ** 2
   909→
   910→    variance = variance_sum / n_chunks
   911→    return max(0.0, 1.0 - min(variance * 4.0, 1.0))
   912→
   913→
   914→@app.post("/flume/encode", response_model=FlumeEncodeResponse)
   915→async def flume_encode(request: FlumeEncodeRequest):
   916→    """Encode a 256D vector through the trained VAE, returning mu and log_var."""
   917→    import torch
   918→
   919→    vae = _get_vae()
   920→    z_dim = vae.config.z_dim
   921→
   922→    if len(request.vector) != z_dim:
   923→        raise HTTPException(
   924→            status_code=422,
   925→            detail=f"Expected {z_dim}D vector, got {len(request.vector)}D",
   926→        )
   927→
   928→    with torch.no_grad():
   929→        x = torch.tensor([request.vector], dtype=torch.float32, device=vae.device)
   930→        h = vae.encoder(x)
   931→        mu = vae.mu_head(h)
   932→        log_var = vae.logvar_head(h)
   933→
   934→    mu_list = mu.squeeze(0).tolist()
   935→    log_var_list = log_var.squeeze(0).tolist()
   936→    coherence = _compute_coherence(mu_list, z_dim)
   937→
   938→    return FlumeEncodeResponse(mu=mu_list, log_var=log_var_list, coherence=coherence)
   939→
   940→
   941→@app.post("/flume/decode", response_model=FlumeDecodeResponse)
   942→async def flume_decode(request: FlumeDecodeRequest):
   943→    """Decode a latent vector through the VAE, returning the reconstruction."""
   944→    import torch
   945→
   946→    vae = _get_vae()
   947→
   948→    with torch.no_grad():
   949→        z = torch.tensor([request.latent], dtype=torch.float32, device=vae.device)
   950→        recon = vae.decoder(z)
   951→
   952→    recon_list = recon.squeeze(0).tolist()
   953→    coherence = _compute_coherence(recon_list, len(recon_list))
   954→
   955→    return FlumeDecodeResponse(reconstruction=recon_list, coherence=coherence)
   956→
   957→
   958→@app.post("/flume/interpolate", response_model=FlumeInterpolateResponse)
   959→async def flume_interpolate(request: FlumeInterpolateRequest):
   960→    """Interpolate between two 256D vectors in latent space."""
   961→    import torch
   962→
   963→    vae = _get_vae()
   964→    z_dim = vae.config.z_dim
   965→
   966→    if len(request.vector_a) != z_dim or len(request.vector_b) != z_dim:
   967→        raise HTTPException(
   968→            status_code=422,
   969→            detail=f"Both vectors must be {z_dim}D",
   970→        )
   971→
   972→    if not 0.0 <= request.ratio <= 1.0:
   973→        raise HTTPException(
   974→            status_code=422,
   975→            detail="Ratio must be between 0.0 and 1.0",
   976→        )
   977→
   978→    with torch.no_grad():
   979→        xa = torch.tensor([request.vector_a], dtype=torch.float32, device=vae.device)
   980→        xb = torch.tensor([request.vector_b], dtype=torch.float32, device=vae.device)
   981→
   982→        # Encode both vectors
   983→        ha = vae.encoder(xa)
   984→        mu_a = vae.mu_head(ha)
   985→        hb = vae.encoder(xb)
   986→        mu_b = vae.mu_head(hb)
   987→
   988→        # Linear interpolation in latent space
   989→        mu_interp = (1.0 - request.ratio) * mu_a + request.ratio * mu_b
   990→
   991→        # Decode the interpolated latent
   992→        result = vae.decoder(mu_interp)
   993→
   994→    result_list = result.squeeze(0).tolist()
   995→    coherence = _compute_coherence(result_list, z_dim)
   996→
   997→    return FlumeInterpolateResponse(
   998→        result=result_list,
   999→        coherence=coherence,
  1000→        mu_a=mu_a.squeeze(0).tolist(),
  1001→        mu_b=mu_b.squeeze(0).tolist(),
  1002→    )

  1162→@app.post("/rl/step", response_model=RlStepResponse)
  1163→async def rl_step(request: RlStepRequest):
  1164→    """Run a single RL step: state -> policy -> action + coherence."""
  1165→    import numpy as np
  1166→
  1167→    if len(request.state) != 256:
  1168→        raise HTTPException(
  1169→            status_code=422,
  1170→            detail=f"State must be 256D, got {len(request.state)}D",
  1171→        )
  1172→
  1173→    policy = _get_rl_policy()
  1174→    state = np.array(request.state, dtype=np.float32)
  1175→    action, _log_prob = policy.get_action(state)
  1176→
  1177→    # Compute coherence of resulting state (state + scaled action)
  1178→    next_state = state + action * 0.01
  1179→    coherence = _compute_coherence(next_state.tolist(), 256)
  1180→
  1181→    return RlStepResponse(
  1182→        action=action.tolist(),
  1183→        coherence=coherence,
  1184→    )

  1187→@app.post("/rl/episode", response_model=RlEpisodeResponse)
  1188→async def rl_episode():
  1189→    """Run a full RL episode (up to 200 steps) with the trained policy."""
  1190→    import gymnasium as gym
  1191→    import numpy as np
  1192→
  1193→    policy = _get_rl_policy()
  1194→    env = gym.make("cohezion/FlumeNav-v0", max_steps=200)
  1195→
  1196→    try:
  1197→        obs, info = env.reset(seed=42)
  1198→        trajectory: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
  1199→        total_reward = 0.0
  1200→        coherences: list[float] = [info["coherence"]]
  1201→
  1202→        for _step in range(200):
  1203→            action, _log_prob = policy.get_action(obs)
  1204→            obs, reward, terminated, truncated, info = env.step(action)
  1205→
  1206→            total_reward += reward
  1207→            coherences.append(info["coherence"])
  1208→            trajectory.append(
  1209→                {
  1210→                    "state_mean": float(np.mean(obs)),
  1211→                    "state_std": float(np.std(obs)),
  1212→                    "action_norm": float(np.linalg.norm(action)),
  1213→                    "reward": reward,
  1214→                    "coherence": info["coherence"],
  1215→                }
  1216→            )
  1217→
  1218→            if terminated or truncated:
  1219→                break
  1220→    finally:
  1221→        env.close()
  1222→
  1223→    return RlEpisodeResponse(
  1224→        steps=len(trajectory),
  1225→        total_reward=total_reward,
  1226→        mean_coherence=float(np.mean(coherences)),
  1227→        final_coherence=coherences[-1],
  1228→        trajectory=trajectory,
  1229→    )

  1294→    # LLM discrete trajectory (5 points)
  1295→    llm_z = [s["physics_state"].get("z", 0) for s in steps]
  1296→    llm_coherence = [s["physics_state"].get("coherence", 0) for s in steps]
  1297→    llm_steps = range(1, len(steps) + 1)
  1298→
  1299→    # CALM continuous trajectory (interpolated 20 points)
  1300→    calm_steps = np.linspace(1, len(steps), 20)
  1301→    calm_z = np.interp(calm_steps, list(llm_steps), llm_z)
  1302→    calm_coherence = np.interp(calm_steps, list(llm_steps), llm_coherence)
  1303→
  1304→    # Add smooth flow (simulate CALM's trajectory prediction)
  1305→    noise = np.random.normal(0, 0.02, len(calm_z))
  1306→    calm_z_smooth = np.convolve(calm_z + noise, np.ones(3) / 3, mode="same")
  1307→    calm_coherence_smooth = np.convolve(calm_coherence, np.ones(3) / 3, mode="same")
  1308→
  1309→    # Calculate smoothness scores

  1459→@app.post("/skills/{skill_name}/execute", response_model=SkillExecuteResponse)
  1460→async def execute_skill(skill_name: str, request: SkillExecuteRequest):
  1461→    """Parse skill, expand instructions into a plan, and execute via PlanExecutor."""
  1462→    from cohezion.agents.factory import AgentFactory
  1463→    from cohezion.core.instruction_expander import InstructionExpander
  1464→    from cohezion.core.plan_executor import PlanExecutor
  1465→    from cohezion.swarm.compound_client import get_compound_client
  1466→
  1467→    factory = AgentFactory()
  1468→    try:
  1469→        spec = factory._resolve_spec(skill_name)
  1470→    except KeyError:
  1471→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Skill not found: {skill_name}") from None
  1472→
  1473→    class_name = f"{spec.name}Agent"
  1474→
  1475→    # Expand instructions into a plan and execute
  1476→    try:
  1477→        expander = InstructionExpander()
  1478→        plan = expander.expand(spec)
  1479→        compound = get_compound_client()
  1480→        executor = PlanExecutor(token_client=compound)
  1481→        exec_result = await executor.execute(plan, request.input_text)
  1482→

  1490→                duration_ms=sr.duration_ms,
  1491→            )
  1492→            for sr in exec_result.steps

  1516→    """Use CapabilityRegistry to find best agents for a query."""
  1517→    from cohezion.registry.capability_registry import CapabilityRegistry

  1519→    registry = CapabilityRegistry()

  1715→@app.get("/metrics/system", response_model=SystemMetricsResponse)
  1716→async def metrics_system():
  1717→    """Return system resource metrics."""
  1718→    import psutil
  1719→
  1720→    mem = psutil.virtual_memory()
  1721→
  1722→    # Check Ollama availability
  1723→    ollama_available = False
  1724→    ollama_models: list[str] = []
  1725→    try:
  1726→        import httpx as _httpx
  1727→
  1728→        async with _httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=2.0) as client:
  1729→            resp = await client.get("http://localhost:11434/api/tags")
  1730→            if resp.status_code == 200:
  1731→                ollama_available = True
  1732→                models_data = resp.json().get("models", [])
  1733→                ollama_models = [m["name"] for m in models_data]

  1737→    return SystemMetricsResponse(
  1738→        cpu_percent=psutil.cpu_percent(interval=0.1),
  1739→        memory_total_gb=round(mem.total / (1024**3), 2),
  1740→        memory_available_gb=round(mem.available / (1024**3), 2),
  1741→        memory_percent=mem.percent,
  1742→        ollama_available=ollama_available,
  1743→        ollama_models=ollama_models,
  1744→    )

  1747→@app.post("/knowledge/query", response_model=KnowledgeQueryResponse)
  1748→async def knowledge_query(request: KnowledgeQueryRequest):

  1750→    from cohezion.knowledge_graph.query_engine import KnowledgeGraphQueryEngine
  1751→
  1752→    engine = KnowledgeGraphQueryEngine()
  1753→    results = engine.search_knowledge(request.query, top_k=request.top_k)

  1769→@app.get("/metrics/tokens", response_model=TokenMetricsResponse)
  1770→async def metrics_tokens():
  1771→    """Return token efficiency metrics from the shared compound client."""
  1772→    from cohezion.swarm.compound_client import get_compound_client
  1773→
  1774→    # Use a module-level override if set, otherwise the compound singleton
  1775→    client = getattr(metrics_tokens, "_client", None)
  1776→    if client is None:
  1777→        client = get_compound_client()
  1778→    return TokenMetricsResponse(**client.get_metrics())

  1780→
  1781→def set_token_client(client: Any) -> None:
  1782→    """Register a TokenEfficientClient for the /metrics/tokens endpoint.
  1783→
  1784→    Pass ``None`` to revert to the default compound client singleton.
  1785→    """
  1786→    metrics_tokens._client = client  # type: ignore[attr-defined]

  1789→# --- Swarm Execution ---
  1790→
  1791→
  1792→class SwarmExecuteRequest(BaseModel):
  1793→    intent: str
  1794→    max_agents: int = 4
  1795→
  1796→
  1797→class SwarmTaskResult(BaseModel):
  1798→    task_id: str = ""
  1799→    subject: str = ""
  1800→    status: str = ""
  1801→    error: str | None = None
  1802→    duration_ms: float = 0.0
  1803→    tokens: int = 0
  1804→
  1805→
  1806→class SwarmExecuteResponse(BaseModel):
  1807→    report_id: str = ""
  1808→    plan_name: str = ""
  1809→    intent: str = ""
  1810→    status: str = ""
  1811→    total_tokens: int = 0
  1812→    total_duration_ms: float = 0.0
  1813→    tasks: list[SwarmTaskResult] = []
  1814→
  1815→
  1816→@app.post("/swarm/execute", response_model=SwarmExecuteResponse)
  1817→async def swarm_execute(request: SwarmExecuteRequest):
  1818→    """Plan and execute a swarm from a natural language intent."""
  1819→    from cohezion.swarm.compound_client import get_compound_client
  1820→    from cohezion.swarm.execution_orchestrator import ExecutionOrchestrator
  1821→    from cohezion.swarm.team_orchestrator import TeamOrchestrator
  1822→
  1823→    compound = get_compound_client()
  1824→    orchestrator_obj = TeamOrchestrator()
  1825→    plan = orchestrator_obj.plan_team(request.intent, max_agents=request.max_agents)
  1826→    executor = ExecutionOrchestrator(token_client=compound)
  1827→    report = await executor.execute(plan)
  1828→
  1829→    report_dict = report.to_dict()
  1830→    return SwarmExecuteResponse(
  1831→        report_id=report_dict.get("report_id", ""),
  1832→        plan_name=report_dict.get("plan_name", ""),
  1833→        intent=report_dict.get("intent", ""),
  1834→        status=report_dict.get("status", ""),
  1835→        total_tokens=report_dict.get("total_tokens", 0),
  1836→        total_duration_ms=report_dict.get("total_duration_ms", 0.0),
  1837→        tasks=[SwarmTaskResult(**t) for t in report_dict.get("tasks", [])],
  1838→    )
  1839→
  1840→
  1841→# --- Compound Engineering Metrics ---
  1842→
  1843→
  1844→class CompoundMetricsResponse(BaseModel):
  1845→    total_learnings: int = 0
  1846→    top_compound_scores: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
  1847→    suggested_refinements: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
  1848→    total_executions: int = 0
  1849→
  1850→
  1851→@app.get("/metrics/compound", response_model=CompoundMetricsResponse)
  1852→async def metrics_compound():
  1853→    """Return compound engineering metrics from retrospection analysis."""
  1854→    from cohezion.compound.metrics import get_collector
  1855→    from cohezion.core.compound.retrospection import RetrospectionEngine
  1856→
  1857→    engine = RetrospectionEngine()
  1858→    learnings = engine.analyze_learnings()
  1859→    scores = engine.calculate_compound_scores()
  1860→    refinements = engine.suggest_skill_refinements()
  1861→
  1862→    top_scores = sorted(scores.items(), key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)[:10]
  1863→    collector = get_collector()
  1864→
  1865→    return CompoundMetricsResponse(
  1866→        total_learnings=len(learnings),
  1867→        top_compound_scores=[{"name": name, "score": score} for name, score in top_scores],
  1868→        suggested_refinements=[
  1869→            {
  1870→                "skill_name": r.skill_name,
  1871→                "reason": r.reason,
  1872→                "learning_count": len(r.suggested_additions),
  1873→            }
  1874→            for r in refinements
  1875→        ],
  1876→        total_executions=collector.total_executions,
  1877→    )
  1878→
  1879→

  1885→class CompoundExecuteRequest(BaseModel):

  1887→    input_text: str
  1888→    model: str | None = None

  1910→@app.post("/compound/execute", response_model=CompoundExecuteResponse)
  1911→async def compound_execute(request: CompoundExecuteRequest):
  1912→    """Execute a PRIME skill with live Ollama models via CompoundExecutor."""
  1913→    from cohezion.compound.executor import get_executor
  1914→
  1915→    executor = get_executor()
  1916→    try:
  1917→        result = await executor.execute_skill(
  1918→            request.skill_name,
  1919→            request.input_text,
  1920→            model=request.model,
  1921→        )
  1922→    except KeyError as exc:
  1923→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Skill not found: {request.skill_name}") from exc
  1924→    except Exception as exc:
  1925→        logger.exception("Compound execution failed: %s", request.skill_name)
  1926→        raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail="Execution failed") from exc
  1927→
  1928→    return CompoundExecuteResponse(
  1929→        skill_name=result.skill_name,
  1930→        final_output=result.final_output,
  1931→        steps=[
  1932→            CompoundStepOut(
  1933→                step_index=s["step_index"],
  1934→                operation=s["operation"],
  1935→                description=s["description"],
  1936→                output=s["output"],
  1937→                tokens_used=s["tokens_used"],
  1938→                duration_ms=s["duration_ms"],
  1939→                model=s.get("model", ""),
  1940→            )
  1941→            for s in result.steps
  1942→        ],
  1943→        total_tokens=result.total_tokens,
  1944→        total_duration_ms=result.total_duration_ms,
  1945→        model_usage=result.model_usage,
  1946→    )

  1949→class CompoundFeedbackRequest(BaseModel):
  1950→    skill_name: str
  1951→    input_text: str
  1952→    model: str | None = None
  1953→    cycles: int = 1
  1954→
  1955→
  1956→class CompoundFeedbackResponse(BaseModel):
  1957→    skill_name: str
  1958→    cycles_completed: int = 0
  1959→    total_tokens: int = 0
  1960→    total_duration_ms: float = 0.0
  1961→    total_refinements: int = 0
  1962→    compound_score_delta: float = 0.0
  1963→    patterns: list[str] = []
  1964→
  1965→
  1966→@app.post("/compound/feedback", response_model=CompoundFeedbackResponse)
  1967→async def compound_feedback(request: CompoundFeedbackRequest):
  1968→    """Run a compound feedback cycle: execute -> analyze -> refine."""
  1969→    from cohezion.compound.feedback_loop import CompoundFeedbackLoop
  1970→
  1971→    loop = CompoundFeedbackLoop()
  1972→    try:
  1973→        if request.cycles > 1:
  1974→            report = await loop.run_multi_cycle(
  1975→                request.skill_name,
  1976→                request.input_text,
  1977→                cycles=request.cycles,
  1978→                model=request.model,
  1979→            )
  1980→            return CompoundFeedbackResponse(
  1981→                skill_name=report.skill_name,
  1982→                cycles_completed=report.total_cycles,
  1983→                total_tokens=report.total_tokens,
  1984→                total_duration_ms=report.total_duration_ms,
  1985→                total_refinements=report.total_refinements,
  1986→                compound_score_delta=report.final_compound_score_delta,
  1987→            )
  1988→        else:
  1989→            result = await loop.run_cycle(
  1990→                request.skill_name,
  1991→                request.input_text,
  1992→                model=request.model,
  1993→            )
  1994→            return CompoundFeedbackResponse(
  1995→                skill_name=result.skill_name,
  1996→                cycles_completed=1,
  1997→                total_tokens=result.execution_tokens,
  1998→                total_duration_ms=result.execution_duration_ms,
  1999→                total_refinements=result.refinements_applied,
  2000→                compound_score_delta=result.compound_score_delta,
  2001→                patterns=result.patterns,
  2002→            )
  2003→    except KeyError as exc:
  2004→        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Skill not found: {request.skill_name}") from exc
  2005→    except Exception as exc:
  2006→        logger.exception("Compound feedback failed: %s", request.skill_name)
  2007→        raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail="Feedback cycle failed") from exc
  2008→

     1→"""Cohezion API Application Setup.
     2→
     3→Updated for API versioning (ARCH-001, ARCH-007).
     4→"""
     5→
     6→from __future__ import annotations
     7→
     8→import logging
     9→import os
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→
    12→from fastapi import FastAPI, Request
    13→from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware
    14→from fastapi.responses import JSONResponse, RedirectResponse
    15→from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles
    16→
    17→from cohezion.security.rate_limiter import get_rate_limiter
    18→
    19→
    20→logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→# Allowed CORS origins from environment, default to localhost only
    24→_CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get("COHEZION_CORS_ORIGINS", "http://localhost:3000,http://localhost:8080").split(",")
    25→
    26→# API Version info
    27→API_VERSION = "1.0.0"
    28→API_TITLE = "Cohezion API"
    29→API_DESCRIPTION = """
    30→Cohezion API - AI Research Lab API with Swarm workflows and MCP tools.
    31→
    32→## API Versioning
    33→
    34→This API is versioned using URI path versioning. Current version: **v1**
    35→
    36→### Versioned Endpoints
    37→All new endpoints are available under `/api/v1/`:
    38→- `/api/v1/health` - Health check
    39→- `/api/v1/mcp/*` - MCP server endpoints
    40→- `/api/v1/swarm/*` - Swarm orchestration
    41→- `/api/v1/compound/*` - Compound engineering
    42→- `/api/v1/anima/*` - Anima narration
    43→- `/api/v1/vault/*` - Vault search
    44→- `/api/v1/metrics/*` - Observability metrics
    45→- `/api/v1/universe/*` - Universe simulation
    46→
    47→### Legacy Endpoints (Deprecated)
    48→Legacy endpoints at root (`/`) redirect to v1 with deprecation headers.
    49→These will be removed in a future version. See the Deprecation headers
    50→in responses for sunset dates.
    51→
    52→### Deprecation Policy
    53→- Legacy endpoints are supported for 6 months after v1 release
    54→- Deprecation headers include `Sunset` date
    55→- Breaking changes will trigger new major version (v2)
    56→"""
    57→
    58→app = FastAPI(
    59→    title=API_TITLE,
    60→    description=API_DESCRIPTION,
    61→    version=API_VERSION,
    62→    docs_url="/api/v1/docs" if os.environ.get("COHEZION_ENV") != "production" else None,
    63→    redoc_url="/api/v1/redoc" if os.environ.get("COHEZION_ENV") != "production" else None,
    64→    openapi_url="/api/v1/openapi.json",
    65→)
    66→
    67→
    68→# Add API info to OpenAPI schema
    69→@app.on_event("startup")
    70→async def add_api_info():
    71→    """Add version information to OpenAPI schema."""
    72→    if app.openapi_schema:
    73→        app.openapi_schema["info"]["x-api-version"] = "v1"
    74→        app.openapi_schema["info"]["x-sunset-date"] = "2026-09-01"
    75→
    76→
    77→# CORS — restricted to configured origins with explicit methods/headers
    78→app.add_middleware(
    79→    CORSMiddleware,
    80→    allow_origins=_CORS_ORIGINS,
    81→    allow_credentials=False,
    82→    allow_methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "OPTIONS"],
    83→    allow_headers=["Content-Type", "Authorization", "X-Agent-Token", "X-API-Version"],
    84→)
    85→
    86→
    87→# Rate limiting middleware
    88→@app.middleware("http")
    89→async def rate_limit_middleware(request: Request, call_next):
    90→    limiter = get_rate_limiter()
    91→    client_ip = request.client.host if request.client else "unknown"
    92→    result = limiter.check(client_ip, request.url.path)
    93→    if not result.allowed:
    94→        return JSONResponse(
    95→            status_code=429,
    96→            headers={
    97→                "Retry-After": str(int(result.reset_after) + 1),
    98→                "X-RateLimit-Limit": str(result.limit),
    99→                "X-RateLimit-Remaining": "0",
   100→            },
   101→            content={"detail": "Rate limit exceeded"},
   102→        )
   103→    response = await call_next(request)
   104→    response.headers["X-RateLimit-Remaining"] = str(result.remaining)
   105→    # Add API version headers
   106→    response.headers["X-API-Version"] = API_VERSION
   107→    response.headers["X-API-Latest"] = "v1"
   108→    return response
   109→
   110→
   111→# Static files
   112→static_dir = Path(__file__).parent / "static"
   113→if static_dir.exists():
   114→    app.mount("/static", StaticFiles(directory=static_dir, html=True), name="static")
   115→
   116→
   117→# Root redirect to v1 docs
   118→@app.get("/")
   119→async def root():
   120→    """Root endpoint - redirects to v1 API documentation."""
   121→    return RedirectResponse(url="/api/v1/docs", status_code=308)
   122→

     1→"""Cohezion API Legacy Router - Redirects to v1 with deprecation warnings.
     2→
     3→All legacy endpoints at root (/) redirect to /api/v1/ equivalents.
     4→This maintains backward compatibility while encouraging migration.
     5→
     6→Traces: [ARCH-001, ARCH-007]
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import logging
    12→from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
    13→from typing import Any
    14→
    15→from fastapi import APIRouter, Request
    16→from fastapi.responses import JSONResponse, RedirectResponse
    17→
    18→
    19→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    20→
    21→# Sunset date for legacy endpoints - 6 months from now
    22→_SUNSET_DATE = datetime.now(UTC) + timedelta(days=180)
    23→_SUNSET_ISO = _SUNSET_DATE.strftime("%a, %d %b %Y %H:%M:%S GMT")
    24→
    25→legacy_router = APIRouter()
    26→
    27→
    28→def _add_deprecation_headers(response: RedirectResponse | JSONResponse) -> None:
    29→    """Add deprecation headers to legacy responses."""
    30→    response.headers["Deprecation"] = "true"
    31→    response.headers["Sunset"] = _SUNSET_ISO
    32→    response.headers["X-API-Version"] = "v1"
    33→    response.headers["X-Deprecation-Notice"] = (
    34→        "This endpoint is deprecated. Use /api/v1/ equivalent. See /api/v1/docs for migration guide."
    35→    )
    36→
    37→
    38→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    39→# System Legacy Routes
    40→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    41→@legacy_router.get("/health")
    42→async def legacy_health(request: Request):
    43→    """Legacy health endpoint - redirects to v1."""
    44→    logger.warning(f"Legacy endpoint accessed: {request.url.path} -> /api/v1/health")
    45→    response = RedirectResponse(url="/api/v1/health", status_code=308)
    46→    _add_deprecation_headers(response)
    47→    return response
    48→
    49→
    50→@legacy_router.get("/")
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/api/singletons.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Lazy-loaded singletons for FLUME VAE and RL policy.\n\nThese are module-level singletons that tests reset via\n``cohezion.api._vae_trainer = None`` (see tests/conftest.py).\nThe thin ``__init__.py`` re-exports them so the attribute path\nremains ``cohezion.api._vae_trainer``.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# FLUME VAE singleton\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n_vae_trainer = None\n\n\ndef _get_vae():\n    \"\"\"Lazy-load the trained FLUME VAE (singleton).\"\"\"\n    global _vae_trainer\n    if _vae_trainer is None:\n        import torch\n\n        from cohezion.flume.training import FlumeVAETrainer\n\n        _vae_trainer = FlumeVAETrainer()\n        ckpt_path = Path(\"data/flume/checkpoints/flume_vae_ep50.pt\")\n        if ckpt_path.exists():\n            try:\n                ckpt = torch.load(ckpt_path, weights_only=True)\n                _vae_trainer.encoder.load_state_dict(ckpt[\"encoder\"])\n                _vae_trainer.mu_head.load_state_dict(ckpt[\"mu_head\"])\n                _vae_trainer.logvar_head.load_state_dict(ckpt[\"logvar_head\"])\n                _vae_trainer.decoder.load_state_dict(ckpt[\"decoder\"])\n                logger.info(\"Loaded FLUME VAE checkpoint: %s\", ckpt_path)\n            except (RuntimeError, KeyError) as e:\n                logger.warning(\n                    \"Failed to load FLUME VAE checkpoint %s (architecture mismatch?); \"\n                    \"using random weights: %s\",\n                    ckpt_path,\n                    str(e),\n                )\n        else:\n            logger.warning(\n                \"No FLUME VAE checkpoint found at %s; using random weights\",\n                ckpt_path,\n            )\n    return _vae_trainer\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# RL policy singleton\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n_rl_policy = None\n\n\ndef _get_rl_policy():\n    \"\"\"Lazy-load the trained RL policy singleton.\"\"\"\n    global _rl_policy\n    if _rl_policy is None:\n        import torch\n\n        from cohezion.rl.trainer import PolicyNetwork\n\n        _rl_policy = PolicyNetwork(state_dim=256, action_dim=256, hidden=128)\n        ckpt_path = Path(\"data/rl/checkpoints/policy_final.pt\")\n        if ckpt_path.exists():\n            _rl_policy.load_state_dict(\n                torch.load(ckpt_path, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=True)\n            )\n            _rl_policy.eval()\n            logger.info(\"Loaded RL policy from %s\", ckpt_path)\n        else:\n            logger.warning(\"No RL checkpoint at %s — using random policy\", ckpt_path)\n    return _rl_policy\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Coherence computation\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _compute_coherence(z: list[float], z_dim: int = 256) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Compute HIHO coherence: 1.0 at mean=0.5, decays with variance.\"\"\"\n    arr = np.array(z)\n    n_chunks = min(12, z_dim)\n    chunk_size = z_dim // n_chunks\n    variance_sum = 0.0\n\n    for c in range(n_chunks):\n        start = c * chunk_size\n        end = (c + 1) * chunk_size if c < n_chunks - 1 else z_dim\n        chunk_mean = float(np.mean(arr[start:end]))\n        variance_sum += (chunk_mean - 0.5) ** 2\n\n    variance = variance_sum / n_chunks\n    return max(0.0, 1.0 - min(variance * 4.0, 1.0))\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Token client override\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef set_token_client(client: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Register a TokenEfficientClient for the /metrics/tokens endpoint.\n\n    Pass ``None`` to revert to the default compound client singleton.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Stash on the routes_legacy module so the endpoint can find it.\n    # Import lazily to avoid circular imports.\n    from cohezion.api import routes_legacy\n\n    routes_legacy._token_client_override = client  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/api/__init__.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Cohezion API - FastAPI server exposing swarm and MCP tools.\n\nThin entry point: creates the app, registers routers, and re-exports\nsingletons for backward compatibility (tests/conftest.py resets them\nvia ``cohezion.api._vae_trainer = None``).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom fastapi import FastAPI, Request\nfrom fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware\nfrom fastapi.responses import JSONResponse, RedirectResponse\nfrom fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles\n\nfrom cohezion.security.rate_limiter import get_rate_limiter\n\n# Re-export singletons so ``cohezion.api._vae_trainer`` still works.\nfrom .singletons import (  # noqa: F401\n    _compute_coherence,\n    _get_rl_policy,\n    _get_vae,\n    _rl_policy,\n    _vae_trainer,\n    set_token_client,\n)\n\nlogging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n_CORS_ORIGINS = os.environ.get(\n    \"COHEZION_CORS_ORIGINS\",\n    \"http://localhost:3000,http://localhost:8080\",\n).split(\",\")\n\napp = FastAPI(\n    title=\"Cohezion API\",\n    description=\"AI Research Lab API - Swarm workflows and MCP tools\",\n    version=\"0.1.0\",\n    docs_url=\"/docs\" if os.environ.get(\"COHEZION_ENV\") != \"production\" else None,\n    redoc_url=\"/redoc\" if os.environ.get(\"COHEZION_ENV\") != \"production\" else None,\n)\n\napp.add_middleware(\n    CORSMiddleware,\n    allow_origins=_CORS_ORIGINS,\n    allow_credentials=False,\n    allow_methods=[\"GET\", \"POST\"],\n    allow_headers=[\"Content-Type\", \"Authorization\", \"X-Agent-Token\"],\n)\n\n\n@app.middleware(\"http\")\nasync def rate_limit_middleware(request: Request, call_next):\n    limiter = get_rate_limiter()\n    client_ip = request.client.host if request.client else \"unknown\"\n    result = limiter.check(client_ip, request.url.path)\n    if not result.allowed:\n        return JSONResponse(\n            status_code=429,\n            headers={\n                \"Retry-After\": str(int(result.reset_after) + 1),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Limit\": str(result.limit),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Remaining\": \"0\",\n            },\n            content={\"detail\": \"Rate limit exceeded\"},\n        )\n    response = await call_next(request)\n    response.headers[\"X-RateLimit-Remaining\"] = str(result.remaining)\n    return response\n\n\nstatic_dir = Path(__file__).parent / \"static\"\nif static_dir.exists():\n    app.mount(\"/static\", StaticFiles(directory=static_dir, html=True), name=\"static\")\n\n\n@app.get(\"/\")\nasync def root():\n    return RedirectResponse(url=\"/static/index.html\")\n\n\n# Register route handlers (legacy flat router).\nfrom .routes_legacy import router as _legacy_router  # noqa: E402\n\napp.include_router(_legacy_router)\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    import uvicorn\n\n    uvicorn.run(app, host=\"0.0.0.0\", port=8080)\n"
}
